### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 隨著法國距離下屆總統大選僅剩六個月，極左翼與政府間的政治對立升溫，然而
法國政府急需推動預算案以控制財政赤字與龐大債務。在內政危機加劇與工會
預告 11 月大罷工的背景下，市場對法國債務可持續性的擔憂升溫，將帶動法德
公債利差維持高檔，進而推升歐元區主權風險溢酬並拖累歐元表現。
 上週路透最新調查顯示，在 100 位受訪經濟學家中，高達 94%預期聯準會將於
10 月會議維持利率在 3.75%-4.00% 不變，惟全員一致預估 12 月將重啟升息。
儘管美國受 AI 投資熱潮帶動、經濟成長強勁，且美伊戰事推升油價突破百元
大關、加徵關稅使 PCE 通膨至少在 2028 年前難以回到 2% 目標，官員仍傾
向審慎行事。不過逾四成專家警告，聯準會恐落後通膨形勢，後續面臨至少再升
息兩次甚至擴大升息幅度的上行風險，預期美元大幅回檔的空間小，逢低承接買
盤依然強勁。
 路透最新調查顯示，受日圓疲軟與中東戰事推升能源及進口成本、加劇通膨壓力

影響，近七成受訪經濟學家預計日本央行將於 12月再度升息至 1.50%，延續 6
月與 9 月以來的較快緊縮步伐；同時，逾半數專家預估終端利率將上看 2.00%
或更高。不過，高達近四分之三受訪者警告，若高市早苗內閣提名的鴿派委員再
次投下反對票，恐壓抑日圓表現。在歐美央行年底前亦可能升息的背景下，日本
央行維持較快的緊縮節奏，有助於防止美日、歐日利差進一步擴大，避免日圓在
年底遭遇新一輪劇烈拋售，為日圓提供實質的下檔保護。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.360，低點在 32.704，以 32.508 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣走貶，周初，外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢，台幣跳空開低，跌破今
年 7 月的低點 32.528；隨著台股表現不佳，外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣續創低位，
最低來到 32.704周內低點；而後台股低檔反彈，外資由匯出轉為雙向進出，加以出
口商積極進場拋售美金，台幣由貶轉升至 32.500下方；臨近周末，市場擔憂颱風來
襲周五可能休市，增添連續假期變數，外資再度大幅賣超台股並匯出，令台幣又跌
回 32.600上方，終場接在央行調節之下收在 32.508。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。近期全球科技股因 AI 類股估值偏高及融資風險疑慮
而持續修正，市場風險偏好降溫，股市面臨修正，外資連續賣超台股並匯出，台幣
走勢持續面臨壓力。另一方面，美伊戰事和談進展的消息反覆，令國際油價節節攀
升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，引發通膨升溫的擔憂，支撐利率維持在高檔的預期，
美元指數也在 101.5 上方高位震盪，將不利台幣的表現。盤勢上來看，外資隨著股
市回檔修正表現偏向賣超台股並匯出，台幣偏弱，不過本周適逢月底，預計匯價逢
高仍持續有出口商拋售美金的需求，將限制台幣走貶的幅度。綜合以上，預估本週
台幣走勢偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣走升。周初中國公布 9月工業生產物價指數 5.5%，表現優於預期的
5.4%(前值 5.2%)，且第三季 GDP 表現 4.5%，也優於預期的 4.4%(前值 4.3%)，基本
面穩健支撐人民幣升破 6.6800 整數關口；不過周間美元指數持續於 101.5 上方高位
震盪，加以美伊戰事未見緩解，國際油價持續飆升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，人民
幣於 6.6800下方高檔震盪；臨近周末，人行中間價格連續走升，升破 6.7200整數關
口，週五最高來到 6.7180，顯示中國人行允許人民幣溫和升值的走勢，終場境外人
民幣升破 6.6700整數關口，收在 6.6690，週線升值 160點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6704，
週線升值 166 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價持續偏升，將持續帶
動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現；就國際市場來看，最
新利率期貨顯示，本周聯準會利率決議有 9成的機率維持利率在 3.75%~4.00%不變，
不過年底前仍有一碼的升息空間，加以地緣政治風險未見緩解，能源價格高檔震盪
推升通膨與利率預期，美元指數維持在 101.5 上方高檔震盪，將限制人民幣升值的
速度跟幅度；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價
維持在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6300-6.6900

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌。週初，USD/JPY 大致在 158 的整數關卡上下波動；
週間，AI相關概念股遭拋售，加之中東局勢緊張推升避險需求，10年期美債殖利率
跌破 5%，避險資金亦流入日圓，帶動 USD/JPY 下行最低來至 156.67；臨近週末，
市場避險需求持續加之日本 9 月通膨數據高於日本央行 2%目標，USD/JPY 一度下
跌至 156.06，後逢低買盤進場跌幅有所收斂，終場收在 156.86，週線下跌 113 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下： 一、美國 10 月 S&P製造業 PMI
為 56.0高於 9 月的 55.9且為 53個月高點，服務業則為 57.9低於 9月的 58.8，綜合
PMI為 57.6亦低於 9 月的 58.4，顯示美國商業活動雖略有放緩但仍處於擴張，經濟
基本面依然穩健將持續支撐美元；此外，中東局勢惡化使能源供應風險上升，布蘭
特原油價格突破 116 美元，為六個月以來高點，也將有利 USD/JPY上行。二、日本
9 月全國整體消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.1%，且 9 月核心 CPI年增 2.1%，為九
個月以來首次超過日本央行 2%的目標，強化市場對日本央行短期升息的預期，預計
為日圓帶來支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY匯價處於各天期均線之
下且接連跌破 158 及 157 關卡，後在 156 整數關卡暫時獲得支撐，顯示逢低仍有買
盤進場，將限縮下行幅度。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較
高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.00-158.50 
前週交易區間 
156.06 158.59

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD震盪下跌。週初，EUR/USD在 1.1200整數關卡窄幅波動；週間
受到油價及美債殖利率回落，外加法國社會局勢略見緩解影響，帶動匯價反彈至
1.1260遇到賣壓後回落，後續大致在 1.1190至 1.1250 間波動；臨近週末，歐元區 10
月份 PMI表現低迷，且市場對於法國政局和財政赤字擔憂持續，拖累 EUR/USD轉
若，匯價一度下行來至 1.1183，終場收在 1.1202，週線下跌 37點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD震盪偏空，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元區 10月 PMI
顯示德、法表現分化，法國製造業及服務業均表現疲弱低於 50榮枯線，拖累歐元區
10 月綜合 PMI 由 9 月 53.1 下滑至 51.9，為四個月以來新低，經濟基本面承壓將不
利 EUR/USD上行；此外，法國社會及政經局勢不穩導致法債殖利率突破 5%，市場
對其財政及政治危機擔憂持續，也將不利歐元表現。二、美國上週初請失業金人數
降至 19.4萬人，為三個月以來新低，顯示勞動力市場依然穩健，且 10月 S&P 製造
業 PMI為 56.0續創 53個月新高，服務業及綜合 PMI維持穩健，顯示美國經濟仍具
韌性，預期將限縮 EUR/USD上行空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
各天期均線維持空頭排列，且匯價持續承壓於月線之下，顯示短線空方趨勢仍未改
變，在能有效突破上方月線前仍以震盪偏空看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週
呈現震盪偏空的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1120-1.1270 
前週交易區間 
1.1180 1.1260

### Page 7

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.19-115.10.22 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.825%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
  公債－10年期- A15109 2.000%~2.002% 
      － 5年期- A15108     1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，儘管市場預期強烈颱風「薔琵」來襲，週五金融市場可能
因颱風假而暫停交易，銀行間多預留帳上資金以應不時之需，但市況仍屬充
裕，即便有新增調度需求，也很容易滿足，令短率朝區間低位移動。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.825%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.68%，利率低位較上週下跌約 1 個基本點。

二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 週四於等殖系統成交在 2.002%，
5 年期指標券 A15108則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.88%。 
週二 10 年期公債標售得標利率 2.001%，落於市場預期區間下緣，並創 2008
年以來新高，顯示市場持續反映經濟成長與通膨預期。 
考量通膨前景及國際債市走勢仍具不確定性，市場觀望氣氛濃厚；惟第四季公
債供給相對有限，可望抑制殖利率大幅彈升。預期短線台債殖利率維持區間整
理，5 年券及 10 年券交易區間分別估於 1.82%~1.84%及 1.98%~2.01%。

Document 078: 2026年11月LPR报价保持稳定，短期内下调可能性不大

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年11月LPR报价保持稳定，短期内下调可能性不大.pdf
Report source: 东方金诚
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
2026年 11月 LPR报价保持稳定，短期内下调可能性不大 
 王青  冯琳 
2026年 11月 20日，中国人民银行授权全国银行间同业拆借中心公布新版 LPR报价：1
年期品种报 3.0%，上月为 3.0%；5年期以上品种报 3.5%，上月为 3.5%。 
解读：

11月两个期限品种的 LPR报价保持不变，符合市场预期。11月初，央行优化短端利率调
控机制，宣布自 11月 2日起每日开展固定利率的隔夜逆回购操作招标，并明确目前操作利率
是 1.25%；7天期逆回购操作调整为多重价位中标，政策利率属性淡化。这意味着，央行主要
政策利率从 7天期向隔夜“换锚”。“换锚”后，DR001 的走廊区间从 1.15%-1.65%下调至
1.0%-1.5%。但需要指出的是，这并非政策利率下调，也意味着 11月 LPR报价的定价基础没
有变化。另外，尽管近期 DR001 等主要货币市场利率、银行同业存单到期收益率、存款利率
都有不同程度的下行，但受贷款利率持续走低等影响，当前银行净息差仍然处在历史低位附
近，报价行缺乏主动下调 LPR报价加点的动力。 
近期 LPR报价保持稳定，根本原因是三季度 GDP增速回升到 4.5%，出口延续高增，以高
技术制造业为代表的新质生产力领域加速发展；10 月宏观数据有所走弱，但更多属于月度正
常波动，四季度经济增长动能仍有上行趋势，完成全年“4.5%至 5.0%”的经济增长目标难度
不大。在此背景下，近期包括全面降息在内的总量货币政策保持定力。 
展望未来，短期内 LPR报价下调的可能性不大。主要原因在于，9月 29日央行调整完善
若干结构性货币政策工具，包括 PSL 利率下调 0.25 个百分点，支农支小再贷款额度上调
5000亿，其中民企再贷款额度上调 3000亿，科技创新和技术改造再贷款额度上调 2000亿，
“报销”比例由原来的 60%上调至 100%；与此同时，财政部门也会同央行、金融监管总局，
首次在全国范围内出台了针对部分首套刚需购房贷款的贴息政策。这意味着逆周期调节已优
先选择结构性政策工具发力。我们判断，在以上结构性稳增长、稳楼市措施出台后，接下来
将进入一段政策效果观察期，短期内全面降息等总量货币政策工具跟进的可能性下降，LPR
报价的定价基础会保持稳定。 
拉长时间来看，综合考虑经济金融演化趋势，预计 2027年央行有望全面降息 10到 20个

东方金诚宏观研究

2 
基点（0.1到 0.2个百分点），届时将引导 LPR报价跟进下调。

Document 121: 261112_daiwa_HH

Source PDF: all report pdf/261112_daiwa_HH.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Hon Hai’s 3Q26 results, announced 12 November. 
We are impressed with its opex control, which led to solid operating margin 
expansion despite gross margin dilution from rising AI server contribution.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat. Net income was TWD78bn (EPS: 
TWD5.53), 25% higher than our estimate (TWD62bn) and 10% higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus (TWD70bn). Revenue was TWD3.0tn (+19% 
QoQ; +46% YoY) with better-than-expected smartphone contribution. The 
gross margin came in at 6.02% (vs. 6.12% in 2Q26), a 0.1pp decline QoQ 
on a rising cloud segment mix. That said, the operating margin expanded 
0.07pp to 3.83%, driven by strong opex control and better leverage.

Key updates: Hon Hai reaffirms its positive view of its 2027 revenue 
momentum, targeting double-digit growth YoY (similar to our forecast). The 
company believes its cloud business will remain a key growth driver (54% 
of total revenue in 3Q26). Regarding total AI server racks, Hon Hai expects 
to see high-double-digit growth in 2027 (vs. our expectation of c.30% YoY 
growth from GPU server plus a rising ASIC contribution; however, as ASIC 
AI servers typically utilise consignment models, its AI server revenue 
growth will be slightly diluted). As for smartphones, considering Hon Hai’s 
key clients’ models command high ASPs, we believe the impact from rising 
memory costs will be relatively smaller, but we expect the PC segment to 
decline YoY due to the negative impact from rising component costs.

During the 3Q26 results call, AI server end demand from the US was the 
hottest question, especially after the US mid-term elections. We believe 
management’s view on hyperscalers’ and neocloud’s near-term demand 
remains positive, but the long-term impact must be closely monitored. Also, 
as investors are worried about the AI server capex slowdown from CSPs, 
management also indicated that there would be new end demand from 
sovereign and enterprise groups, which could act as a second growth 
wave. For 4Q26, the company is guiding for >15% revenue growth QoQ 
and YoY given the ICT peak season along with rising AI server revenue (up 
by 5-15% QoQ due to ongoing AI server shipment strength).

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 10-12% to reflect 
higher ASP assumptions for AI servers. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating but 
trim the 12M TP to TWD320, using a lower target PER of 13x (prior 16x; vs. 
its past-3-year range of 8-18x) on our 1-year forward EPS, given the overall 
weaker sentiment. Downside risk: low demand for AI servers.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E revenue are above the consensus by 2-3%, 
likely as we are more optimistic on Hon Hai’s AI server shipments.

12 November 2026

3Q26 review: solid opex control

➢ OPM expansion on strong scale leverage despite GM contraction  
➢ Demand outlook remains promising even after US mid-term elections 
➢ Reiterating Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD320

Company profile 
Established in 1974, Hon Hai Precision Industry (Hon Hai) started out making plastic products, 
connector products and cable assemblies. It is now the largest electronics manufacturing service (EMS) 
provider globally in terms of design, manufacturing, global logistics and after-market service.

### Page 5

5

Hon Hai Precision Industry (2317 TT): 12 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 12 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Hon Hai has incorporated 4 independent members into its 7-member board of directors in pursuit
of better governance. However, the company's Chairman also serves concurrently as President,
implying an absence of separation between board leadership and executive management.
Capital management 1 Hon Hai has declared >40% payout ratio for most of the years since 2014, and >50% in the past 5 years, pointing to a shareholder-friendly dividend policy.
Related party &
transaction 2
Revenue from related parties accounted for 1.6% of total revenue for 2025, while purchases
from related parties constituted 0.1% of cost of sales. We believe there is limited risk from
related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2 Hon Hai adheres to green design concepts and incorporates environmental factors and pollution prevention measures during the design and development stages. The company's green product design guidelines include 4 axes: "energy-saving, non-harmful, reduction and easily recyclable."
S Labor practices 2
Hon Hai recognises employees as the most important asset and continues to invest in its
workforce. In terms of safety, Hon Hai has upgraded its occupational safety and health
management system since 2019, and obtained the ISO45001 and CNS45001 certificates. The
company held online activities related to factory safety and health. Overall, we note that Hon Hai
has improved in terms of labour management in recent years. Hon Hai established a team to
implement data security management. Moreover, it plans to conduct data security training and
education regularly for all employees. Hon Hai aims to achieve zero data security incident in the
future.
S Product design & lifecycle management 2 Hon Hai adheres to green design concepts and incorporates environmental factors and pollution prevention measures during the design and development stages. The company's green product design guidelines include 4 axes: "energy-saving, non-harmful, reduction and easily recyclable."
Company specific Management Analyst comments
E GHG emissions 2
In November 2020, Hon Hai announced its commitment to achieving net zero greenhouse gas
emissions by 2050. Its Scope 1 and 2 GHG emissions decreased by 53.6% in 2025 compared
with 2020, exceeding its reduction target of 21%. That said, the company's actual energy-saving
rate in mainland China was 5.2% in 2025, successfully achieving its energy-saving goal of 4.2%.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
Limited direct impact on Aspeed and Montage in the near term,

but we see potential long-term demand risks.
The US BIS has tightened export controls to restrict Chinese companies' remote
access to advanced computing capacity through third-country data centers, while
adding licensing requirements for advanced chips shipped to key Southeast Asian
markets, including Singapore and Malaysia (link).
Our view: Gary Yu (China Internet Analyst; link) sees this as another step toward
closing loopholes in Al compute access, potentially slowing GPU deployment by

China-linked cloud operators and raising compliance costs for overseas Al
infrastructure investment. New supplies of advanced chipsets within China to
become increasingly limited. For cloud semis, we see limited direct impact on
Aspeed and Montage in the near term but there are potential long-term demand
risks. Aspeed has a high share in GPU and China server platforms. Montage
interface and retimer chips may also be impacted over the long term.
Please also refer to our recent notes: Cloud Semis: Peak Growth, Less Upside;

Investor pushback on our cloud semis/old memory downgrades.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

3217.TT 優群 
 消費電子偏弱，靜待伺服器新品放量

[財務資訊更新]   
   公司簡介 
優群成立於 1987 年，總部位於新竹，主要從事電子零組件及連接器的研發、設計與
製造，產品涵蓋 DDR、M.2、USB Type-C 及微型沖壓件，應用於 PC、伺服器、網通、
手機及車用電子等領域，並積極布局高頻高速傳輸的連接器技術。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
區間操作 114 
【前次投資建議】買進，234 
【大盤指數/股價】36033 / 95.4

➢ 3Q26 記憶體價格高漲致客戶短料，EPS 2.84 元，低於預期 
3Q26 營收 9.75 億元，QoQ+2.9%、YoY-13.8%，受 DRAM 缺貨漲價
促使筆電採用板載設計影響，SO-DIMM 及 M.2 銷售年減 36%及 20%。
CAMM 出貨雖季增 10 倍，仍不足以彌補缺口；毛利率 46.06%，QoQ-
1.5ppts，YoY-5.1ppts，主因產能利用率下降及產品組合轉差；EPS 2.84
元，YoY-32.8%。營收與獲利皆低於投研部預期。 
➢ 4Q26 消費電子續弱但伺服器占比漸升，衰退幅度將逐步收斂 
10 月營收 2.58 億元，MoM-22.8%、YoY-25.9%，主要受筆電及消費性
電子需求疲弱影響。展望 4Q26，Long-DIMM、Type-C、微型沖壓件可
望延續 3Q26 的成長，伺服器占比將逐步增加，此外電信通訊產品亦可
望挹注營收。投研部預估 4Q26 營收 8.25 億元，QoQ-15.4%、YoY-
20.7%，毛利率 44.75%，QoQ-1.3ppts，YoY-4.2ppts，但衰退幅度將
收斂；稅後 EPS 2.31 元，QoQ-18.4%，YoY-28.4%。 
➢ 2027 Google 伺服器新案受矚，營運有望重返成長 
展望 2027 年，優群將積極降低對筆電市場的依賴，預估筆電營收占比
由 60%降至 50%，伺服器與桌機合計提升至 20%，電信通訊增至 10%。
主要成長動能來自 Google 伺服器新專案預計 1Q27 量產、越南廠導入
Long-DIMM 及微型沖 壓 件，以及電信通訊產品 持 續放量。此外，
CAMM2 受惠 HP 新機於 1H27 陸續導入，可望帶動出貨；SOCAMM2
則已進入客戶送樣測試階段，若順利通過驗證，有望切入 AI 伺服器供應
鏈，進一步提升高階產品營收比重。 
➢ 預估 2027 年 EPS 11.47 元，建議區間偏操作，目標價 114 元 
預估 2026 年/2027 年營收 36.95 億元/40.46 億元，YoY-10.9% /+9.5%；
毛利率為 46.88% /47.31%，YoY-3ppts /+0.4ppts；稅後 EPS 10.35 元
/11.47 元，YoY-18.6% /+11%，目前 PER 約在 9.2X /8.3X，參考過往
PER 區間多位於 10X~18X，目前股價以 2027 年衡量低於區間下緣，公
司雖因 Google 新案產品將於 1Q27 量產而貢獻伺服器營收，但消費電
子成長力道仍偏弱，且近期整體盤勢波動劇烈，故投研部建議區間偏多
操作，目標價 114 元(2027F PE 10X)。

3217 優群 加權指數

### Page 2

- 2 -

個股報告

3Q26 記憶體價格高漲衝擊插槽需求且客戶模組短缺，EPS 2.84 元，低於預期 
優群 3Q26 營收 9.75 億元，QoQ+2.9%，YoY-13.8%，雖受季節性因素而季增，但
低於投研部預期並呈現年衰退，因記憶體原廠優先供應 AI 伺服器與 HBM，導致一般
DRAM 缺貨漲價，迫使筆電品牌改採直接焊接的板載(On-board)設計，排擠記憶體
插槽需求。在此衝擊下，主力產品 SO-DIMM 與 M.2 銷售額 YoY 分別為-36%與-
20%，再加上次世代 CAMM 連接器出貨雖季增 10 倍，仍受客戶端記憶體模組短缺
限制，無法完全填補傳統產品線缺口；毛利率 46.06%，QoQ-1.5ppts，YoY-5.1ppts，
營收規模縮減直接導致工廠產能利用率下降，單位固定製造成本分攤墊高、越南新廠
為初期營運而不具規模優勢，以及傳統高毛利的 SO-DIMM 銷售比重縮水但 Type-C
與微型沖壓件等其他成長中的產品線在組合變化下，拖累整體毛利率；稅後 EPS 2.84
元，QoQ+18.9%，YoY-32.8%，亦低於預期。 
圖 1：優群產品營收佔比(依季度)

資料來源：國票投顧整理 
4Q26 消費電子續弱但伺服器占比逐步上升，衰退幅度將逐步收斂 
優群 10 月營收為 2.58 億元，MoM-22.8%、YoY-25.9%，月營收明顯低於 3Q26 單
月平均水準，主因受筆電及消費性電子需求下滑衝擊，客戶拉貨動能轉弱，使公司於
4Q26 初的出貨表現相對保守。展望後續，Long-DIMM、Type-C 連接器及微型沖壓
件等產品可望延續 3Q26 的成長趨勢，伺服器與桌機相關業務亦逐步增加；此外，電
信通訊產品已於 2026 年 9 月開始出貨，預期將持續挹注營收，成為目前新的成長動
能。投研部預估，隨著 11 月及 12 月營收年減幅度較 10 月收斂，4Q26 營收為 8.25
億元，QoQ-15.4%，YoY-20.7%；因筆電相關產品出貨減少，使工廠稼動率下降，固
定製造成本分攤效率轉差，加上產品組合改變及部分客戶要求價格支援，使毛利率持
續承壓，預估為 44.75%，QoQ-1.3ppts，YoY-4.2ppts；稅後 EPS 2.31 元，QoQ-
18.4%，YoY-28.4%。 
優群開發多項新品，領域拓展至 AI PC 與伺服器所需的高頻高速連接器 
優群目前開發中的產品眾多，包含如下： 
1) LPDDR5 CAMM2 666p SMT Solutions：CAMM2 為新一代壓接式記憶體模組架
構，主要應用於高階筆電、AI PC 及工作站，而公司開發的 666-pin SMT 連接器方
案，搭配上蓋與背板設計，可強化模組固定與接觸穩定性，並支援高密度記憶體配置，
相較傳統 SO-DIMM，CAMM2 有助於提升主機板空間利用效率及記憶體擴充彈性。

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
2) LP5 SOCAMM2：應用於 AI 伺服器與高效能運算(HPC)的新型壓接式記憶體模組，
採用 LPDDR5 記憶體，兼具低功耗、高頻寬與模組化設計等特性，而公司開發的
SOCAMM2 連接器採 694-pin、1.0mm 高度規格，可支援高密度記憶體配置，並具
備模組拆裝及維護彈性，未來有機會隨 AI 伺服器平台升級而擴大應用。 
3) M.2 Gen 6：新一代 M.2 高速連接器，主要對應未來高效能 SSD 及高速儲存裝置
的介面升級需求。隨著 AI 運算及資料中心持續擴大，系統對儲存裝置的傳輸頻寬、訊
號完整性與連接穩定性要求也將提高，促使連接器朝更高速規格發展，而公司目前已
將 M.2 Gen 6 列為開發中產品，預期可延續既有 M.2 Gen 4、Gen 5 的技術與客戶基
礎，布局下一世代高速儲存市場。 
4) DDR DIMM Socket - DDR6 426p：此插槽為因應下一世代 DDR6 記憶體規格所
開發的插槽連接器，主要應用於未來伺服器及高效能運算平台。隨著記憶體傳輸速度
與資料頻寬持續提升，連接器必須具備更高的訊號完整性、接觸可靠度及機構穩定性，
而公司具備 DDR5 RDIMM、UDIMM 插槽開發經驗，目前正在開發 426-pin DDR6 
DIMM Socket。 
5) 7A×48V (336W)大電流/高功率型 Type-C：公司針對高功耗設備開發的連接器產
品，透過提升端子導電能力與散熱性能，支援最高 7A 電流及 48V 電壓，理論供電功
率達 336W，高於 USB PD 3.1 EPR 標準的 240W，可望應用於高效能筆電、工作站、
伺服器及工業設備，滿足高功率供電與快速充電需求。 
6)可拆卸式/模組化 Type-C 連接器：採用連接器與轉接座成對使用的設計，提供板上
式與沉板式兩種組合，客戶可依產品內部空間與機構需求彈性選用，此設計除支援
USB-C 高速訊號傳輸外，也具備模組化、更換便利及配置彈性等優勢，可降低維修與
更換成本，並滿足筆電、伺服器及新世代電子設備對高速傳輸與輕薄化設計的需求。 
整體而言，這些產品以布局高階筆電、AI 伺服器、高速儲存與次世代記憶體市場為主，
反映優群正由傳統 NB 用 SO-DIMM、M.2 連接器，逐步拓展至 AI PC 與伺服器所需
的高密度、高頻高速連接器。 
2027 Google 伺服器新案受矚，營運有望重返成長 
展望 2027 年，優群的營運結構可望逐步擺脫過去高度依賴筆電市場的情況，並朝伺
服器、電信通訊及高階記憶體連接器等領域轉型。由於 2027 年筆電市場仍可能受到
記憶體缺料、價格上漲及終端需求疲弱等因素影響，品牌客戶對筆電產品的拉貨態度
預期仍相對保守，筆電相關營收恐面臨衰退壓力，因此公司將積極調整產品組合，降
低單一應用市場波動對整體營運的影響。投研部預估，筆電營收占比將由目前約 60%
下降至 2027 年的 50%，桌機與伺服器合計占比則由 14%提升至 20%，電信通訊占
比由 5%提高至 10%，OSAT 占比亦可望由約 12%提升至 12.5%，其中伺服器與電信
通訊兩項業務合計占比將達 30%，成為 2027 年營收成長的重要支撐。在伺服器業務
方面，公司既有 CSP 客戶包括 Meta 與 Google，其中 Google 新專案預計於 2026
年 11 月進行試產，並於 1Q27 透過越南廠正式量產，由於該專案規模相對較大，預
期隨著訂單逐步放量，將對伺服器相關營收帶來實質挹注。目前越南廠已承接 DDR5 
SO-DIMM 及 M.2 等標準產品，未來則將陸續導入 Long-DIMM、Google 伺服器新
專案及微型沖壓件，並規劃於 2H27 逐步將微型沖壓件產能移入越南廠，使越南廠的
產品布局由既有記憶體及儲存裝置連接器，進一步延伸至伺服器相關產品，藉此擴大
生產規模、提升產能利用率，同時配合客戶實踐 China+1 策略及供應鏈分散化的需
求，強化公司在國際伺服器供應鏈中的競爭優勢。

### Page 4

- 4 -

個股報告 
此外，電信通訊也是優群 2027 年值得關注的成長動能，公司相關產品已於 2026 年
9 月開始出貨，4Q26 持續貢獻營收，代表該業務已逐步由前期產品開發及客戶導入
階段進入實際出貨期，預期 2027 年隨著客戶需求擴大及訂單持續增加，營收貢獻將
進一步提升，故投研部預估其占比將由 2026 年的 5%倍增至 2027 年的 10%，顯示
電信通訊產品有機會成為公司未來降低筆電業務依賴、改善營收結構的重要成長來
源。 
另一方面，高階記憶體連接器方面，CAMM2 及 SOCAMM2 也是優群未來值得追蹤
的產品。CAMM2 主要應用於高階筆電、工作站及部分新型記憶體架構，公司於 3Q26
出貨量已較前期大幅成長，且 HP 多款新機種預計於 1Q27 至 2Q27 陸續導入，有助
於帶動後續出貨；至於 SOCAMM2，則是公司持續布局的高階伺服器記憶體連接器，
主要受惠於 AI 伺服器對高頻寬、高密度及高效能記憶體架構的需求提升，目前產品
已進入客戶送樣與測試階段，未來若能順利通過大型 CSP 客戶驗證並取得正式訂單，
將有機會進一步切入 AI 伺服器供應鏈，為公司帶來新的產品成長動能。 
總體而言，優群 2027 年的營運成長將主要來自伺服器與電信通訊業務擴張、Google
新專案量產，以及 CAMM2 等高階記憶體連接器持續導入，其中 Google 專案與電信
通訊產品具有相對明確的出貨時程，而 SOCAMM2 則屬於中長期成長機會；若越南
廠新產品導入順利，加上 CSP 客戶訂單持續增加，將有助於公司逐步降低筆電市場景
氣波動的影響。

毛利率為 46.88% /47.31%，YoY-3ppts /+0.4ppts；稅後 EPS 10.35 元/11.47 元，
YoY-18.6% /+11%，目前 PER 約在 9.2X /8.3X，參考過去五年 PER 區間多位於
10X~18X，目前股價以 2027 年衡量低於區間下緣，儘管公司因 Google 新案產品將
於 1Q27 量產，帶動伺服器占比升至 2 成，但考量消費電子相關營收成長力道仍偏
弱，且近期因市場對 AI 前景存有疑慮致整體盤勢波動劇烈，故投研部建議區間偏多
操作，目標價 114 元(2027F PE 10X)。

4971.TT IET-KY 
 400G 磊晶、QD Laser 的先鋒

[法說會更新]   
   公司簡介 
英特磊科技股份有限公司(IET-KY)於 2011 年 4 月設立，為全球磷化銦(InP)、銻化鎵
(GaAs)、銻化鎵(GaSb)磊晶片代工領導廠，提供無線通訊、光纖通訊等產品應用。3Q26
營收組成為 GaAs 36.5%、InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它 3.5%。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
區間操作 424 
【前次投資建議】無 
【大盤指數/股價】43092 / 400

➢ 3Q26 出貨量回升，4Q26 認列設備營收，2026 年營收年增 17.4% 
由於 InP 基板供貨恢復穩定使 Epi-Wafer 出貨量成長，IET-KY 3Q26 營
收 3.2 億元(YoY+26.8%，QoQ+24.6%)。毛利率 38.7%(YoY+7ppt)提
升係因高毛利水準之磊晶片及硬體銷售所帶動。EPS 1.26 元。3Q26 營
收組成為 GaAs 36.5%、InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它
3.5%。各項產品線營運狀況如下：(1)GaAs：射頻與國防應用合計占其
比重約 20%，營收年增 72%。(2)InP：APD/PD 需求極為強勁，但因基
板供應受限，營收年減 2%。考量客製化修改特殊設備營收預計於 12 月
認列，且基板供貨穩定，研究員預期 12 月營收高於 10~11 月，預計
4Q26 營收季增 12.4%至 3.6 億元(YoY+12.7%)，預估 2026 年營收 12.7
億元(YoY+17.4%)，毛利率 40%，EPS 4.85 元。 
➢ 2027 年 400G EAM 與 Driver 開始量產，產能年增 10%至 20% 
展望 2027 年，GaAs 方面，國防訂單與商用訂單同步增加 30%。VCSEL
在新增機台後產能估約增加 40%，預估 GaAs 營收年增 20%至 25%。
第一位客戶已突破 InP 基板限制後，預計 2027 年開始量產 400G EAM
與 Driver。若 2027 年基板供應能維持現有水準，預 期 InP Epi-Wafer 出
貨量將有雙位數成長。在 GaSb 方面，公司著重於生物辨識等創新應用，
並已建置第二條 GaSb 生產線，預計於 2027 年正式量產。配合晶片法
案承諾，公司將於 2027 年購買 2 至 3 台機台，機台總數增至 18 台至
19 台，總產能約增加 10%至 20%，預估 2027 年營收 16.1 億元
(YoY+27.1%)，毛利率 41.6%，EPS 6.40 元。 
➢ 初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元 
IET-KY 作為全球唯一一家採用 MBE 製程量產磊晶的供應商，客戶包含
多家全球雷射大廠。在產品規格上，公司已踏入 400G EAM 與 Driver、
200G VCSEL、QD Laser 等高規格產品所用之 Epi-Wafer 的研發，且
400G EAM 與 Driver、QD Laser 於 2027 年就會開始出貨，產品進程遠
遠領先同業。然而，在 400G 產品面臨龐大需求時，成本較低的 MOCVD
勢必取代初期採用的 MBE 成為主要技術。參考光通訊產業 Forward PE
評價多落在 20X~50X 區間，以 2028 年 EPS 換算，目前為 49X，評價
偏高。初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元(2028F PE 52X)

個股報告

公司介紹 
英特磊科技股份有限公司(IET-KY)於 2011 年 4 月設立，為全球磷化銦(InP)、銻化鎵
(GaAs)、銻化鎵(GaSb)磊晶片代工領導廠，是少數採用 MBE 技術從事生產的磊晶供
應商，具有基板到磊晶的垂直整合技術能力，提供無線通訊、衛星通訊、光纖通訊等
商業用途，以及太空與國防上的紅外線感測、夜視、攝影等產品應用。3Q26 營收組
成為 GaAs 36.5%、InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它 3.5%。 
資料來源:國票投顧 
圖 1：IET-KY 營收組成

3Q26 出貨量回升，4Q26 認列設備營收，2026 年營收年增 17.4% 
由於 InP 基板供貨恢復穩定使 Epi-Wafer 出貨量成長，IET-KY 3Q26 營收 3.2 億元
(YoY+26.8%，QoQ+24.6%)。毛利率季增 4.7ppt 至 38.7%(YoY+7ppt)提升係因高
毛利水準之磊晶片及硬體銷售所帶動。EPS 1.26 元。3Q26 營收組成為 GaAs 36.5%、
InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它 3.5%。應用比重為射頻 19.6%、汽車防
撞及車用 13.3%、光纖網路 24.6%、高速傳輸元件 13.7%、紅外線偵測 25.2%、硬體
構件 3.6%。各項產品線營運狀況如下：(1)GaAs：射頻與國防應用合計占其比重約
20%，營收年增 72%。(2)InP：受惠於 5G 基礎建設、高頻光纖網路與雲端運算發展，
APD/PD 需求極為強勁，100G/200G PD 占整體營收 15%至 20%，但因基板供應受
限，營收年減 2%。(3)GaSb：由於國防客戶快速加單，商用訂單持續擴大，3Q26 營
收占比由 15%升至 26.5%，營收年增 18%。考量客製化修改特殊設備營收預計於 12
月認列，且基板供貨穩定，研究員認為 11 月與 10 月營收水準差異不大，12 月營收
高於 11 月，預計 4Q26 營收季增 12.4%至 3.6 億元(YoY+12.7%)，預估 2026 年營
收 12.7 億元(YoY+17.4%)，毛利率 40%，EPS 4.85 元。 
2027 年 m-PIN、400G EAM 與 Driver 開始量產，產能年增 10%至 20% 
展望 2027 年，各產品線發展如下：(1)GaAs：國防訂單與商用訂單同步增加 30%。
m-PIN(變晶型 PIN)可望進入量產階段。VCSEL 在新增機台後產能估約增加 40%。二
次長晶已由淡水廠接單，新機台即將完成驗收，已獲得固定客戶下單並進行認證，2027
年可支應客戶需求，預估 GaAs 營收年增 20%至 25%。(2)InP：第一位客戶已突破 InP

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
基板限制，預計 2027 年開始量產 400G EAM 與 Driver。在歐洲供應商導入，以及研
磨、晶片表面拋光重複使用等技術逐漸成熟下，若 2027 年基板供應能維持現有水準，
預期 InP Epi-Wafer 出貨量將有雙位數成長。(3)GaSb：公司著重於生物辨識等創新
應用，並已建置第二條 GaSb 生產線，預計於 2027 年正式量產。在公司新增機台後，
GaSb 產能估增加 30%。(4)勞務收入(硬體構件)：客製化新材料機台代營運及「由租
轉買」合約已接近完成。產能方面，在二期廠房擴建工程方面，經過審核與跨政黨行
政團隊過渡，法律與技術問題已全數解決，德州晶片法案補助款亦已陸續領取並支應
擴建工程，預計 4Q26 內完成簽署正式合約，1Q27 完工，可望於 2H27 開始挹注營
收。同時，配合晶片法案承諾，公司將於 2027 年購買 2 至 3 台機台，機台總數增至
18 台至 19 台，總產能約增加 10%至 20%。新增機台將優先配置於 VCSEL 與 GaSb。
假設工廠平均稼動率保持在 70%至 80%，與 2026 年相近，預估 2027 年營收 16.1
億元(YoY+27.1%)，毛利率在 400G EAM 與 Driver、VCSEL 等高毛利產品營收比重
上升下增至 41.6%，EPS 6.40 元。 
IET-KY 可望成為全球第三家 QD Laser Epi-Wafer 供應商 
展望 2028 年，IET-KY 於 2027 年進入試產階段的 QD Laser GaAs Epi-Wafer 可望開
始放量出貨，成為繼 QD Laser Inc.及 Innolume GmbH 之後，全球第三家 QD Laser 
Epi-Wafer 供應商。200G VCSEL 可望從研發階段步入試產或量產階段。在 InP 部分，
第二位 400G EAM 與 Driver 客戶產品將開始量產，與第一位客戶一同貢獻 InP 營收，
加上 1.6T 之 200G PD 出貨比重提升，預期 InP 營收仍會有雙位數成長。生物辨識所
需的 GaSb Epi-Wafer 則因市場持續擴大而出貨年增雙位數，預估 2028 年營收 19.3
億元(YoY+19.8%)，毛利率 42.4%，EPS 8.16 元。 
MBE 是下一代規格的起源，初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元 
IET-KY 作為全球唯一一家採用 MBE 製程量產磊晶的供應商，客戶包含多家全球雷射
大廠。在產品規格上，已踏入 400G EAM 與 Driver、200G VCSEL、QD Laser 等高
規格產品所用之 Epi-Wafer 的研發，且 400G EAM 與 Driver、QD Laser 於 2027 年
就會開始出貨，產品進程遠遠領先同業。然而，在 400G 等產品面臨龐大需求時，成
本較低的 MOCVD 勢必取代初期採用的 MBE 成為主要技術，因此 IET-KY 也將維持
「營收規模小、毛利高」的路線，以前沿技術出貨高規格產品而穩定成長。參考光通
訊產業 Forward PE 評價多落在 20X~50X 區間，以 2028 年 EPS 換算，目前為 49X，
評價偏高。初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元(2028F PE 52X)。

Document 350: Global Markets Outlook 2027_ Growing Pains

Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

We have published our 2027 Macro Outlook: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks. We flesh 
out the Global Market implications and key asset views here, highlighting 10 
investment themes that are likely to dominate price action in 2027. More detailed 
outlooks for the individual asset classes are forthcoming.  
A maturing market cycle: Stable global growth and declining inflation make for 1.
a friendly macro baseline, but risks are to the downside as the market cycle ages. 
The AI investment impulse peaks: AI has contributed more to market gains 2.

### Page 3

Markets Outlook 2027: Growing Pains

1. A maturing market cycle 
Stable growth, declining inflation make for a friendly macro backdrop. n
But risks are to the downside, and an ageing market cycle is facing more headwinds. n
Higher rates, wider spreads, and a peaking AI impulse mean the broad asset picture n
is more two-sided ... 
... and argues for more diversified allocations, including bonds and hedges. n
Our headline macro forecasts for 2027 are quite friendly: stable growth, declining 
inflation and peaking policy rates. But as the economic cycle extends, the market cycle is 
increasingly showing its age. For 2026, our view was that even though asset market 
valuations were running ahead of the macro, a positive cyclical backdrop including 
strong investment growth should dominate. While the Iran war has upended many 
forecasts, even without the energy shock the market is running into more conventional 
late-cycle headwinds. Real rates are higher, credit spreads are wider and volatility is 
picking up, especially under the surface. On top of this, the AI impulse, which has been a 
key support to markets through the shocks this year, looks poised to peak in the coming 
year. 
That is likely to prove a bigger headwind to the profit and asset market picture than 
growth, given its larger role in supporting equity markets. Markets have already moved 
some way towards reflecting that the extraordinary surge in profitability in the AI 
infrastructure complex is unlikely to be extrapolated. So a flattening in the gains there 
may be offset by gains further down the waterfall among AI users or other cyclical parts 
of the market, especially if energy prices and rates fall more. But that is a tricky 
balancing act, and further boosts to equity prices are likely to come alongside a higher 
base level of volatility. There are also risks that AI-related drawdowns tighten financial 
conditions more meaningfully, or that energy and policy relief do not arrive on time. 
That leaves the broader risk-asset picture looking more two-sided than it has for several 
years, arguing for more diversified and better-hedged portfolios. 
12 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 4

2. The AI investment impulse peaks 
The AI investment impulse peak is a bigger test for markets than the economy. n
That impulse has been a larger contributor to growth in market value and earnings n
than in GDP. 
Markets have already de-rated AI infrastructure beneficiaries. n
Valuation excesses are less visible, so the focus is on sustainability of record profits. n
Coming into 2026, we expected continued acceleration in AI investment to dominate 
valuation concerns in equities, but likely amid higher volatility and wider credit spreads.  
Although we expect solid AI investment growth to continue, our forecasts imply that the 
investment growth impulse—additional AI investment as a share of GDP—will peak in 
early 2027. From a macro perspective, this should be a manageable event. But it may be 
a bigger test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger 
contributor to growth in earnings and market value than to growth in GDP. Second, 
AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity gains, especially 
companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have made them more 
vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the point at which 
growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters, even if underlying 
growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through that peak allows 
markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and, as growth 
decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as we may 
be starting to see for the AI capex cycle. 
Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant de-rating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are 
most sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. Moreover, robust profits, and a rising share of

valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this cycle is 
whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in profits, 
which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove persistent, 
especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation allowed during that 
tech cycle—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure 
on the cost of capital.

3. Passing—or dropping—the baton 
Strong levels of AI investment growth and profitability should limit the pressure. n
And the baton may be passed from AI infrastructure to other market areas. n
But we see risks of a larger aggregate shock if the market looks forward, or other n
areas cannot take up the slack. 
Tighter financial conditions or credit conditions could create a feedback loop. n
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition through the peak of 
the investment cycle will prove to be. The first is that although the investment impulse 
from AI looks poised to peak, growth in both AI investment and earnings should remain 
firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth 
to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a still-robust 11% in 2027. This may ultimately help to 
limit the de-rating of even the most directly affected areas and should reduce the 
impact on broader growth-sensitive assets, though it should also imply less relief in core 
rates pricing. The second is that the baton may be passed, at least to a degree, from the 
direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies that may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies that see some relief as the crowding out of capital

The risk is that what could be more of a rotation—and a flattening in the upward trend in 
asset markets—becomes a bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the 
market begins to look further forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 
on our forecasts); because the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place 
as quickly or as smoothly as needed; or because feedback loops from financial and 
credit conditions accelerate more quickly than in our baseline. We think the risk to 
markets is higher than the risk to the economic picture on each front, because the boom 
itself has had its largest impact there. For that reason, the most likely channel through 
which AI worries could lead to broader economic downside is through financial 
conditions themselves.

4. Energy—balancing risk and risk premium 
An escalation-driven oil spike remains one of the largest near-term risks to markets. n
Our baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices, but the hedge value of energy n
is still high. 
Downside tail to energy prices with clearer/faster resolution. n
Risk premium has fluctuated, so hedge value also varies over time. n
A potential oil price spike driven by another escalation in the war with Iran remains one 
of the biggest near-term risks to the markets and growth picture. Particularly after the 
fall in oil prices so far in November, pricing is vulnerable to any broadening in the conflict 
and new supply disruptions. We would expect that to hurt equities, support the Dollar 
against European currencies and put upward pressure on rates, all else equal. Europe, 
too, faces a precarious winter, given low storage levels in natural gas which leave the 
region vulnerable to risks from both supply and weather. If those risks are avoided, our 
baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices over the coming year even without full 
resolution of the conflict. Despite the ongoing war with Iran, oil market supply has 
largely been restored (even if refining capacity is limited), but risks of escalation and 
further disruptions have generally led the market to demand a fluctuating but 
meaningful risk premium above where we think the fundamental supply-demand picture 
would leave them.

Our baseline is a gradual recovery in supply and reduction in energy prices, leaving 
energy and core commodity returns more muted. But the risk of more serious 
disruptions is real, and energy prices could move significantly higher in those scenarios 
at a time when equities and, potentially, bonds could both be under pressure. The 
hedging value of the asset class remains high for that reason. The wrinkle is that the risk 
premium in oil has varied substantially and conditions the value of the hedge. When the 
risk premium falls, as it has done more recently, that hedging value is likely to be higher. 
When that risk premium is very high, as it was at the end of October, oil markets are 
more vulnerable to downside tails if news flow is better than expected, and outright 
longs (rather than options) have generally been more risky. The downside tail from faster 
progress towards resolution than we expect (not just in Iran, but in Ukraine too) is one of 
the clearest sources of upside risks for broader markets. In that scenario, energy prices 
could come down more quickly, supporting both bonds and equities.

5. Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value 
Rates moved up in 2026, on oil, hikes, AI spending and fiscal deficits. n
Central bank pricing now too hawkish relative to our baseline. n
Bonds should be a better hedge as supply shocks give way to demand stresses. n
Higher running yield, more paths to relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher n
bond allocations. 
Interest rates moved higher in 2026 after a low point on the eve of the Iran war. The 
resultant surge in energy prices and central bank pricing has been the primary catalyst 
across most DM and EM rates markets, but real rates, especially in the US, have 
continued to move higher as investment demand from the AI build-out interacts with 
sustained fiscal deficits. Despite that backdrop, we think that central bank pricing in 
many economies, including the US and the UK, is too hawkish relative to what is likely to 
be delivered. Even with the energy shock, the underlying inflation story is still fairly 
benign, which means that policy rates are likely to peak at lower levels relative to 
2023-2024 and reverse earlier. The risk to this view is that the energy shock extends 
further or inflation remains stickier and the labour market proves more resilient, as it did 
this year. 
Bonds have been a poor hedge in this environment of energy supply shocks, rate hikes

and real rate pressures from private and public sector spending. But we think the case 
for duration has shifted in a more constructive direction for several reasons. First, on a 
static basis, bond yields are now providing a good running yield and a decent spread to 
cash. Second, valuations are cheap (we see UST 10y yields at 4.6% by end-2027), 
positioning is underweight, and there are paths to some shallow rate relief from current 
levels under our benign baseline scenario, and scope for larger moves if AI spending 
decelerates more quickly. Third, the increase in yields has started to create stresses that 
indicate that a higher level of real rates may be difficult to sustain. Bonds are already 
starting to illustrate their value as a hedge against shocks of these types—with Bunds 
rallying as French sovereign spreads widened and USTs rallying as tech stocks came 
under pressure. Although energy spikes remain a near-term risk, we are more positive 
about bond allocations even after the recent rally, in part because of that hedging value.

6. A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies 
After tight ranges in 2026, lower inflation, energy prices make for a softer Dollar. n
But a bigger USD swing could come if US tech exceptionalism erodes. n
JPY is a more attractive hedge with ‘team Japan’ focused on limiting Yen weakness. n
CNY appreciation has further to run given undervaluation and a surging trade n
surplus.  
The broad Dollar is finishing 2026 on the strong side of tight ranges, buoyed by a sturdy 
US nominal growth backdrop, higher energy prices, and a shift to more hawkish Fed 
pricing. Under our baseline forecast, all three impulses should fade: alongside trend 
growth we expect inflation in the US to converge towards target, energy prices should 
decline as the Middle East disruptions gradually normalize and we expect the Fed to 
move from hikes to cuts as we head into late 2027 and early 2028. That should make for

a softer Dollar backdrop, and we have pencilled in a roughly 3% fall in the broad 
trade-weighted Dollar in 2027. 
But the real swing factor for FX is likely to be how the peak in AI-related tech spending 
affects US equity returns. Over the past several years, tech-related macro and market 
outperformance in the US has sucked in equity capital flows from the rest of the world 
and has been the primary factor supporting the Dollar’s premium valuation. Our 
baseline forecasts envisage a more even distribution of returns globally rather than US 
underperformance. But if US tech performance erodes meaningfully, that would likely 
come alongside some cyclical underperformance of the Dollar rather than a traditional 
‘safe haven’ bid, as we have already seen in short periods over the past couple of years. 
Instead, we would expect the undervalued Yen to prove a better haven in that scenario, 
given its typical sensitivity to equities and rates falling together. With Japanese policy 
authorities also now focused on limiting further Yen depreciation and encouraging 
inflows into Japanese assets, we think that the Yen should increasingly be a more 
attractive hedge to pro-cyclical portfolios. We also see scope for the Yuan to continue to 
appreciate gradually as China’s trade surplus keeps growing, and forward markets 
underestimate how far this process can run given the deep undervaluation starting 
point. While domestic demand is also very weak, we have argued that China needs both 
currency appreciation and expansionary policy, two instruments to hit two targets, to 
move towards better external and internal balance.

European markets have been under pressure from energy ToT, higher global yields n
and sovereign stresses. 
Even if global shocks abate, local concerns still persist until the French election, n
keeping EUR downside attractive.

### Page 10

UK equities are resilient and defensive, Sterling has continued to strengthen. n
UK Gilts may be clearer outperformers given their highest beta to oil prices. n
European macro markets have had a tough year, primarily reflecting a negative terms of
trade shock, rate tightening without an offsetting investment spending boom, and most
recently the reappearance of sovereign stresses, especially in France. Even with the
repricing, there are still challenges: the energy risks are likely to be most acute going

through the winter, and the underlying fiscal issues in France are unlikely to be
adequately addressed until the French election in the Spring of 2027. As a result, using
the Euro as a funder for other crosses has been a popular position, and the skew of risks
and low carry continue to make downside optionality attractive for the near term. While
Europe remains hostage to global shocks, the main paths to relief also run through those
global channels—energy relief would be most helpful, as would lower yields in the rest of

core markets. Together they would also lessen the immediate pressure on fiscal budgets,
even though the underlying primary deficit would still need to be addressed. Any
slowdown in AI-related spending may also show European assets in a better relative light
given a much smaller share of tech in the region’s equity indices.
In those scenarios, the UK may shine even brighter. Gilts have had the highest beta to oil
prices, so a reduction in those pressures may provide the most relief to UK rates and to

the fiscal picture there. UK equity indices also have less tech exposure than the US
(although they do embed significant energy exposure), and so may also prove more
defensive in an AI-related spending slowdown and any associated market pressures. And
while the currency may also benefit from a softer Dollar backdrop, it is already the most
overvalued currency within our G10 coverage. Much has gone right for the Pound in
recent months—a smooth transition to a new Labour government, a ‘do-no-harm’

budget delivery, the BoE shifting to hikes, and encouraging talk of a closer settlement
with the EU. But we think further gains are likely to be more challenging, with our
monetary policy forecasts most dovish to market pricing in the UK.

8. The stress of financing the boom 
AI investment financing has driven credit and equity issuance to cycle records. n
This has been digestible so far, but with discounted pricing needed in credit that has n
led spreads wider. 
A further widening in US spreads looks likely and a higher chance of meaningful n
widening than meaningful tightening. 
Europe offers more risk premium; mortgages and structured credit give some refuge n
from corporate supply. 
Credit and equity issuance has been hitting fresh cycle records, and the financing needs 
of the AI investment boom mean that those needs are likely to build further in 2027. So 
far, that issuance has—for the most part—been digestible, albeit with discounted 
pricing, and the credit underperformance that we expected in 2026 because of these 
dynamics has only really become clear in the last few months. Balance sheets of the 
major borrowers are strong and while free cash flow positions for the hyperscalers have 
deteriorated sharply, the overall corporate sector is much more balanced. We expect 
continued bifurcation between AI-related issuers and “everything else”, with episodic 
indigestion of jumbo AI debt financing leading to wider spreads for AI-related sectors.  
We expect further widening in index-level credit spreads in 2027, though in our base 
case the scope is fairly modest. The risks around that view are skewed though. It is hard 
to see scenarios where US credit spreads tighten sharply (there is more scope for 
tightening in Europe where the risk premium has increased more), and easier to see how 
spreads could move wider, particularly if economic conditions deteriorate more than we 
expect or if investors become more uncomfortable about the profitability of the AI 
investment boom. We expect modest US HY underperformance (decompression) in our

baseline, but the risk is that problems in the left tail of weaker borrowers create more 
turbulent conditions for broader financing. Mortgages and structured credit markets 
may offer some refuge from the heavy corporate supply. From 1998 to 2000, there was 
sustained widening in credit spreads and substantial decoupling between credit and 
equity markets. Without the large capital inflows and goods price deflation from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998, we think that kind of divergence will be harder now.

9. EM—resilience and diversification 
2026 is one more year when EM assets have shown resilience/outperformance in the n
face of external shocks. 
But the flip side is less compelling valuations and lower carry, despite friendly macro. n
So the case for EM investing is exposure to high, diversified and resilient yields in EM n
fixed income ... 
... and a regional and sectorally diversified earnings mix in EM equities.  n
EM assets have again demonstrated impressive resilience in 2026 in the face of shocks 
from high energy prices and the spikes in core rates. EM rates have outperformed core 
rates, EM FX total returns are outpacing G9 and EM equities are on track for a narrower 
but still double-digit year after 30% gains in 2025. The upshot of this resilience and 
outperformance is that in many places valuations are less compelling than before. EM 
local rate spreads are tight relative to core rates, EM credit spreads are the tightest in 
years despite attractive all-in yields, and EM FX carry and valuations are firmly 
middle-of-the-road. 
Even though the baseline macro backdrop looks friendly for EM investing and 
carry-harvesting in 2027, less attractive valuations and carry levels mean that it is about 
gaining exposure to high diversified yields in both hard and local currency fixed income. 
In many cases, such as South Africa, Hungary, Colombia and India, those higher yields 
come with improving fiscal and structural reform stories. Likewise, in the case of EM 
equities, while the major markets are not expensive, much of the value in the headline 
index is an artefact of the surge in AI earnings that will be hard to repeat. Rather, the 
case for EM equity length is also about gaining exposure to a diversified earnings mix.

### Page 13

The EM equity index, especially the less concentrated, equal-weighted flavours, provide 
exposure not just to the tech and AI complex in North Asia, but also to cyclical and 
commodity-related growth in South Africa and LatAm, and to larger domestic demand 
stories such as India. And if we see a larger move lower in core rates or the Dollar than 
our baseline expectations, EM local assets provide upside convexity that is harder to 
replicate elsewhere.

10. Growth risks and tail hedges 
A benign baseline, but growth risks now skew to the downside. n
Diversification benefits from bonds, commodities and equity selection. n
Equity index volatility has scope to rise further. n
Downside in rates, USD/JPY, upside in energy and protection in equity options. n
With a steady economic backdrop, our baseline forecasts for 2027 are for positive 
returns across equities and bonds. But with a maturing market cycle and significant 
uncertainty around core drivers of the outlook, we continue to emphasize the value of 
diversification and hedges to protect against key risks. Some of those risks—from higher 
oil prices or sticky core inflation—are likely to push rates higher. But a growing number—
a sharper reversal in the AI boom, cybersecurity fears, AI-related labour-shedding or 
financial stresses—would push both growth and rate expectations lower. The further we 
move into 2027, the more likely it is that the key risks are positive for bonds, as the 
threats from central bank hikes, energy price spikes and the AI investment tailwind 
recede. Diversification remains valuable in this environment. As we have said, the value 
of bonds as a diversifying asset is returning as the skew of risks shifts. And 
commodities—especially energy—continue to play a hedging role for portfolios, 
particularly when implied risk premia fall as they have recently.  
Alongside diversification, direct hedges are worth considering. Positioning for lower 
rates in the US and Japan, or for jointly lower rates and equities are potential ways to 
protect against deeper downside growth tails. In FX, USD/JPY downside may play a 
similar role, given that it generally performs well when rates and equities are both falling

broadly. For near-term energy upside, hedges in European gas futures and TTF call 
spreads look like asymmetric ways to protect against risk, as does EUR funding. So far, 
the sharpest volatility has been under the surface of the major equity indices. Equity 
index volatility has risen a little but is unlikely to fall much from here, and we think has 
scope to rise further if macro stresses broaden. Deeper protection in equities 
themselves—either through out-of-the-money puts or replacing equity holdings with 
longer-dated call options—also seems more valuable than usual as the cycle matures.

► 近一週美股、日股及新興市場表現遜於港股
及歐股，因市場擔憂民主黨全面掌控國會恐

便總統川普表態接受共和黨敗選結果並願尋
求民 主 黨 的跨 黨 合 作，仍 無 法緩 解 市 場擔
憂，美股主要指數普遍收跌；歐股方面，面
對全球科技股評價修正，部分資金轉入歐洲

費、醫療保健、公用事業等防禦性產業。

熱門議題
► 黃金價格歷經第二季修整，近日呈現大幅反
彈走勢，主要係因美國期中選舉後，市場憂
心AI產業監管將趨嚴，加上晶片與科技股遭
遇大幅修正，促使部分資金轉往黃金等防禦
型資產尋求避險。
► 根據世界黃金協會最新報告，第3季全球央行
淨買進321公噸黃金，較第二季明顯回升，年
增率達42%，創下自2024年第四季以來的單
季新高，前3季累計買進金額更是高達675公
噸，顯示黃金儲備已經列入全球央行必需資
產配置而且比重維持穩定增長態勢。
► 長期以來黃金具有抗通膨特性，觀察黃金與
聯邦基金利率走勢，從2020年之後，即便全
球歷經2次升息循環，黃金價格仍呈現穩健上
揚走勢，反應其抗通膨保值特性。此外，美
國財政赤字壓力難消，恐令美元長期購買力
被稀釋， 加 速 全 球 央 行 去 美 元 化 ， 有 鑑 於
此，建議黃金納入資產配置。

資料來源：Bloomberg，Factset，凱基證券整理；註：右上產業回報相關性為各產業過去3年之每週報酬相關性
► 醫療類股於美國第三季財報展現強勁的獲利
韌性，實際獲利優於預期程度為主要產業中
表現最佳，背後主要受惠大型製藥企業(如：
禮來、再生元製藥、吉利德科學)在減重藥、
癌症治療及創新藥領域之需求持續增長，帶
動整體產業獲利表現優於市場預期。
► 期中選舉後醫療政策輪廓逐漸明朗。民主黨
主張支持擴大醫療保險覆蓋、延續平價醫療
法案補貼以及緩和醫療補助計劃(Medicaid)
削減，有助提升醫療服務使用率；雖藥價談
判政策恐帶來部分定價壓力，但整體政策主
張仍對創新藥、生技研發及醫療保險產業具
潛在政策支持效果。
► 綜上，在AI投資回報率面臨市場檢驗之際，
市場資金持續尋求防禦性配置。醫療保健類
股與資訊科技類股相關性偏低，有助分散投
資組合對單一AI題材依賴，加上第三季獲利
動能轉強、創新藥需求穩健及產業能見度提
升，後續有望持續受惠資金輪動效應。

政策議題 民主黨傾向 共和黨傾向 兩黨共識對製藥產業潛在影響
降低藥價 依《通膨削減法案》框架，擴大醫療 補助計劃(Medicaid)藥價談判適用範圍 傾向透過最惠國定價機制來降低藥 價 兩黨均主張降低藥價 製藥企業面臨定價壓力
美國本土製造 透過研發補助、稅收優惠及產業政策 鼓勵藥廠回流美國生產 透過關稅、供應鏈重組及企業承諾 推動製藥產能回流 兩黨均支持醫藥供應鏈自主化 有利本土藥廠及原料藥供應鏈投資

支持延長《平價醫療法案》補貼、擴
大醫療補助計劃(Medicaid)覆蓋範圍，
並緩和該補助計劃的削減
傾向由州政府及市場機制主導醫療
體系，限制聯邦政府支出
醫療的可負擔性為兩黨政策共同焦點，有助
增加就醫人口及處方藥使用量，支撐製藥、

投資焦點必需消費
► 短期而言，檢視各類股在最近股災表現，美
國成長股、資訊科技、與通訊服務等高波動
度類股，表現明顯落後大盤，其中，資訊科
技與通訊服務波動度最大、跌幅最重，主要
係因美股科技巨頭財報不如預期、負自由現
金流量，疊加期中選舉後民主黨恐緊縮AI基
礎建設進程及資本支出見頂等不確定因素，
令市場 擔憂 導致 資 金流出 ；並轉 進核 心消
費、健康護理、防禦股、公用事業等防禦性
產業，報酬相對較穩健，受市場資金青睞。
► 長期而言，從2008迄今，核心消費類股長期
表現穩健上漲。與主要指數相比，面對近月
AI科技股修正，同期間費半大跌18%、那斯
達克跌幅6%、標普跌幅1%，而核心消費類
股大漲13%，反應其低波動、抗震韌性。
► 整體而言，從2015年至今，歷經2次通膨與
升息循環，核心消費受影響性低，有鑑於資
產配置角度，建議短期跟隨資金流向配置核
心消費股，提高投組抗震韌性。
必需消費長期表現穩健、抗通膨、低波動避險資金流入，必需消費類股單月表現最佳

配置必需消費類股，提高投組抗震韌性

股市
 隨著美國期中選舉結束，美伊衝突可望降溫，市場關注焦點將回歸基本面，惟AI資料中心建設進程
或將受到州政府緊縮政策而造成評價面壓力增添股價波動。短期可布局防禦股、分散配置於不同國
家與產業，增強抗震力、降低市場波動影響，長期而言，AI從全面看好變成要追求財務健全度較高
的品質股、且大優於小，震盪之後長期正向趨勢不變。
 歐、日股可分散配置，利率走升有利銀行股。此外，隨財政政策支持，歐洲國防軍工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，AI從
全面看好變成要追求財務健全度較高的品
質股、且大優於小，震盪之後長期正向趨
勢不變，非AI部分可關注必需消費產業、
醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 隨資金湧入，公債殖利率回落，惟根據最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升
息空間，立場偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨發展。建議以中短天期債券投資為主，可逢利率走升
時布局進行鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引

力者，包含金融、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 聯準會12月份會議升息態度偏鷹，美元高檔整理。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期偏鷹，日圓弱勢盤整。
美元高檔整理，日圓弱勢盤整
商品
 美國期中選舉結束，預期中東地緣衝突降溫，油價從近期高點回落，短線仍偏震盪。
 去美元化支撐全球央行買盤，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
低接買盤流入，黃金低點反彈，中長期維
持偏多看法

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Document 380: Manus联创张涛：免费补贴是AI应用创业的最大陷阱，要面向“六个月后的模型”做创新

Source PDF: all report pdf/Manus联创张涛：免费补贴是AI应用创业的最大陷阱，要面向“六个月后的模型”做创新.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

张涛在北京⼤学演讲指出， AI 应⽤创业应⾯向 6 个⽉后的模型外溢能⼒设计产品，⽽⾮修补
当下的模型能⼒； 1 秒延迟的视频⽣成模型出现时，可能成为新⼀代操作系统。他还提醒，
免费补贴是 AI 时代最⼤的陷阱，它制造⽆效反馈、扭曲产品判断，让⽤⼾真实感受到智能
才是 AI 产品的价值。 “ 我们被上⼀代移动互联⽹创业的免费补贴陷阱给迷惑了。 ”
摘要：
近⽇， Manus 联合创始⼈、⾸席产品官张涛在北京⼤学 AI 创投课堂发表了⼀场⻓达两⼩时的演
讲，披露了这家 30 ⼈公司从 0 到 1 的创业决策链条。在这场演讲中，张涛抛出了⼀个在当前 AI 创
业圈颇具争议的判断：免费补贴是 AI 应⽤创业最⼤的陷阱，中国 AI 创业者可能在被上⼀代移动互
联⽹的打法所误导。
张涛在演讲中透露， Manus 发布后第⼀个⽉的 ARR （年度经常性收⼊）即突破 4000 万元⼈⺠
币，接近 5000 万，核⼼⽤⼾中不乏每⽉在平台消费 4000 ⾄ 5000 美元的企业管理者。他将这⼀成
绩归结于⼀个核⼼判断：在 AI 时代，让⽤⼾真实感受到智能，才是产品最重要的价值，⽽⾮靠补

贴堆砌 DAU 。
砍掉 7 个⽉⼼⾎：⼀个关于时间成本的战略判断
在 Manus 诞⽣之前，有⼀段鲜为⼈知的弯路。
2024 年 3 ⽉，张涛与联合创始⼈、⾸席科学家 Pick 加⼊肖弘（ Red ）的团队后，团队的第⼀个⽅
向并⾮ Agent ，⽽是⼀款 AI 原⽣浏览器。逻辑顺理成章：公司此前的产品 Monica 是浏览器插件，
天花板明显， " 做浏览器插件出⾝的公司，很容易推演到下⼀个产品应该做个浏览器 " 。
这款浏览器做了整整 7 个⽉，到 2024 年 10 ⽉已接近发布状态。张涛坦⾔， " 发出来应该对市场还
是有点惊艳的 " 。但肖弘最终拍板将其 sunset （放弃）。
我觉得回看过去两三年，中国 AI 应⽤创业，我觉得有⼀个⾮常⼤的陷阱，就是我们被上⼀代移动
互联⽹创业的免费补贴陷阱给迷惑了，以为这⼀代还要这么做，完全错误。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

核⼼原因有两个。其⼀，产品体验的嫁接困境：所有⼈拿到 AI 浏览器后，第⼀个问题都是 " 我在
Chrome ⾥⽤的功能，在你这⾥去哪找 " ，⽤⼾永远⽤旧体验的基准线来衡量新产品，⽽创业团队
根本⽆⼒在浏览器基础功能上对⻬苹果和 Google 。其⼆，时间成本的判断：⼀旦发布，团队将
被迫在这个⽅向上耗费 3 ⾄ 6 个⽉，⽽ " 如果你在创业，钱是最不值钱的东西，浪费了时间才是最
可怕的 " 。
张涛事后评价，这是 Manus 历史上最重要的⼀个决定， " 如果当时不是因为 sunset 了那个浏览器
产品，就没有后来的 Manus" 。
看⻅拐点：从 Cursor ⾥读出的不是 Coding 机会
2024 年 7 ⽉， Cursor 爆⽕。市场上随即涌现出⼤量 Coding Agent 的跟随者。张涛和 Pick 看到的却
是另⼀件事。
" 我们发现 Sonnet 3.5 V2 解锁的不是单纯的 Coding 能⼒，⽽是⼀个 agentic 能⼒。 " 张涛说，这包
括两个维度： Long Horizon Planning （⻓程规划） —— 模型开始主动将任务拆解为多步骤，⽽

⾮在单轮对话中强⾏完成；以及⼤幅增强的 Tool Use （⼯具调⽤）能⼒。
在张涛看来，这个能⼒解锁的对象不是⼯程师，⽽是普通⼈。 " 这个世界不需要更多的 engineer
了，⽽且 engineer 也不需要更多 fancy 的⼯具了。 " 他们看到的机会是：通过 Coding 作为中介，去
解决普通⽤⼾的通⽤场景问题。
2024 年 12 ⽉， Manus 正式⽴项。两个半⽉后， 2025 年 3 ⽉ 5 ⽇发布。发布当天，张涛才发
现， " 好像全世界只有我们在做这个，⼤家都还是在做 Coding" 。
这背后有⼀套 Pick ⼀直坚持的架构哲学，张涛将其概括为 "Less structure, more intelligence" ：
Manus 的 Honeycomb 架构从第⼀版起就完全抛弃了 workflow ，每⼀步的思考和决策全部交由模
型⾃主决定， " 不管你让 Manus 做 research 、做 slides 、做⽹站、写数据分析，所有的事情都是
Manus ⾃⼰决定下⼀步⼲什么的 " 。

这个赌注在 2025 年 2 ⽉中旬 Anthropic 发布 Claude 3.7 的当晚得到验证。张涛在即刻发⽂ " 信仰充
值的⼀晚 "——Honeycomb ⼀⾏代码未改，仅将模型 endpoint 从 3.5 V2 切换⾄ 3.7 ， benchmark 上
剩余未解决的问题 " 百分之⼆三⼗，全部⼀下就解了 " 。
反对 “ 互联⽹式烧钱 ” ：免费补贴是 AI 创业最⼤的陷阱
Manus 发布时，连付费功能都没有做。3 ⽉ 5 ⽇上线，发现⽕爆程度超出预期，团队才从零开始
搭建定价体系，到 3 ⽉ 20 多号才上线付费功能。
⽽在这中间，邀请码成了救命稻草。原因极为现实：公司账上只剩⼋九百万美元，在有邀请码
限制的情况下，单⽇最⾼烧掉 50 万美元。 " 如果没有邀请码，我们公司从 3 ⽉ 5 号发布，可能就⼀
撑⼀个星期， 3 ⽉ 12 号就可以关⻔了。 "
但张涛想说的不只是这段险情，⽽是⼀个更根本的判断：AI 时代，免费补贴是错误⽅向。
我觉得回看过去两三年，中国 AI 应⽤创业有⼀个⾮常⼤的陷阱，就是我们被上⼀代移动互联⽹创

业的免费补贴陷阱给迷惑了，以为这⼀代还要这么做，完全错误。
为什么？因为 AI 这个时代，智能本⾝，如果说你的 performance 本⾝是⾜够好的，⼀定会有⼈
愿意付费的，⼀定是有⼈愿意付费的。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
他的逻辑是：免费补贴吸引来的是⽆效⽤⼾，这些⽤⼾的反馈会迷惑创业者的注意⼒；⽽付费
⽤⼾是⽤钱投票的⽤⼾，他们的需求才是真实需求。 " 你围绕那个需求去做，你就能吸引更多愿
意付费的⼈。 "
他还特别点出⼤⼚的困境：上线第⼀天拿⼀两千万预算补贴⽤⼾，有了⼀⼆⼗万 DAU 之后，没
有⼈敢在第⼆个⽉转向收费， " 它就⼀直拖累了你对于真实⽤⼾市场的感知能⼒ " 。
Manus ⽬前最满意且付费最多的⽤⼾群体，是企业管理者 ——CEO 、⼩⽼板、部⻔主管。张涛
的解释直接： "General AI Agent ， General 通⽤，在公司⾥⾯谁的屁事最多？⽼板嘛。 "
下⼀个转折点在哪⾥：⾯向 “6 个⽉后的模型 ”
张涛在演讲中给出了⼀个具体的创业⽅向预判，他称之为 "1 秒钟延迟的 Sora 时刻 " 。
" 我⼀直在等待⼀个时刻，就是从你输出 prompt 到你看到画⾯延迟⼩于 1 秒钟。我觉得当这个
turning point 到来的时候，会是这⼀轮 AI 整个应⽤创业⾮常重要的转折点。 "

他的判断是， 1 秒延迟的视频⽣成模型已经不是视频⽣成模型，" 它就是新⼀代的操作系统 "。届
时的交互⽅式将从单向内容消费变为每个⼈⾃主参与的互动创作， " 它已经不是单纯的⼀⼈制
作、多⼈消费了 " 。他认为这⼀时刻 "6 个⽉之内⼀定会发⽣ " ，并⿎励创业者现在就开始围绕这个
⽅向准备产品形态。
这⼀判断背后，是张涛对 AI 创业⽅法论的⼀贯主张：永远⾯向 6 个⽉后的模型外溢能⼒去创新，
⽽不是基于今天的模型能⼒修修补补。 " 你应该想象的是 6 个⽉之后模型会变成什么样⼦。如果 6
个⽉之后模型是那个样⼦，我会⽤什么样的产品形态去匹配那个时候的模型。 "
他同时强调，坚持做 General （通⽤）是 Manus 的另⼀个关键战略判断。 " 品牌⼼智的认知是很
难花钱去买的，你在⽤⼾⼼中的 Mindset ，是花⼏亿美⾦都很难改变的事情。 " 正因为⼀开始占住
了 " 什么都能⼲ " 的⼼智， Manus 在后续推出 WebDev 、 Slides 、 Video 等⼦功能时都顺理成章，⽽

那些从垂直场景起家的竞品，想转向通⽤任务时则⾯临⽤⼾认知的强烈阻⼒。
在资本效率问题上，张涛透露， Manus 做到 20 亿美元估值时，历史上总共花掉的融资额约为
5000 ⾄ 6000 万美元。 " 我们团队可能是国内 AI startup ⾥资本利⽤效率最⾼的⼀⽀团队。 " 他的结
论是： AI 创业与其他创业没有本质区别， " 不是因为加了个 AI 的定语，你就可以很理所当然地亏
钱 " 。

### Page 4

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

潜台词：扛不住了，得提价了

Document 393: OpenAI又想叫暂停

Source PDF: all report pdf/OpenAI又想叫暂停.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

OpenAI ⾸席科学家罕⻅公开内部训练数据，提出以 “ 困惑度 ” 为核⼼建⽴⾏业能⼒⻔槛，主
张各家前沿模型公司在安全监控达标前不得突破约定上限。 CEO 奥特曼⾼调背书，却遭⽹
友质疑：这究竟是真⼼护航 AI 安全，还是在技术落后时为⾃⼰争取时间的障眼法？
摘要：
模型能⼒越强，讨论 “ 暂缓 ” 的声⾳就越多。
美国当地时间 11 ⽉ 22 ⽇， OpenAI ⾸席科学家雅库布 · 帕乔茨基（ Jakub Pachocki ）发表了题为
《 Perplexity and Intelligence 》（困惑度与智能）的⽂字，公开公司内部追踪多年的预训练数
据，并提出建⽴ AI ⾏业共同标准。
帕乔茨基希望各家前沿模型公司使⽤统⼀指标衡量模型能⼒，在安全监控能⼒达到要求之前，
不突破约定的能⼒⻔槛。
这项提议也得到了 OpenAI CEO ⼭姆 · 奥特曼（ Sam Altman ）的⽀持。
01
⼀个与智能有关的数字
帕乔茨基这篇⽂章的核⼼，是所谓的 “ 困惑度 ” （ Perplexity ）。

帕乔茨基将模型预测 token 的机制称为训练⼀个压缩器（ Compressor ） —— 模型从数据中提取
规律，⽤更少的信息准确预测接下来会发⽣什么。困惑度衡量的就是这种预测能⼒。
模 型 预 测 得 越 准 确 ， 困 惑 度 越 低 。 OpenAI 实 际 公 布 的 是 对 数 困 惑 度 ， 也 就 是 交 叉 熵 损 失
（ Cross-Entropy Loss ），数值同样是越低越好。
按照帕乔茨基公布的内部数据，GPT-3.5 的对数困惑度约为 0.99 ， GPT-4 约为 0.91。前者已经能
够⽀持流畅的对话，后者则开始有效利⽤思维链（ Chain of Thought ， CoT ）进⾏推理。到了最
新阶段的 GPT-6 版本之后，困惑度指标已降⾄ 0.8 以下。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

帕乔茨基表⽰，模型在数学、⽹络安全等领域开始表现出新的能⼒。其中⼀个变化发⽣在对数
困惑度降⾄约 0.76—— 研究⼈员发现，在这个节点上模型开始连续解决⼀些具有挑战性的数学问
题。
帕乔茨基提到，研究⼈员曾经在 Slack 中看到模型接连解决⾃⼰思考多年的问题。这些表现并⾮
针对某⼀道题进⾏专⻔训练，⽽是模型底层能⼒提升后出现的结果。
帕乔茨基强调，这也是他重视困惑度的原因。
关键原因在于，这项数值不与模型能否完成任务直接关联，⽽是反映它从训练数据中学习信息
的能⼒发⽣了什么变化。
我们过去经常讨论智能涌现，困惑度持续下降带来的能⼒进化，就可以理解为⼀种涌现。
02
2027 年助⼒预训练突破
以往，模型训练⽅法主要由⼈类研究⼈员提出，再通过⼤量实验验证。 AI 开始参与研究后，情况
可能发⽣变化：模型不仅执⾏研究⼈员安排的任务，还可能帮助寻找新的算法、提出实验⽅
案，甚⾄对训练⽅法本⾝作出贡献。
他预计，最早到 2027 年 AI 就可能在预训练领域取得⼀系列基础性进展。最新模型已经开始在研

发过程中提供实质性的设计贡献。
帕乔茨基透露， OpenAI 今年已经观察到预训练损失下降速度明显加快。不过，他也特别说明，
现阶段主要来⾃⻓期研究和算⼒投⼊， AI ⾃动化⽬前只贡献了较⼩部分。
帕乔茨基还预计，随着对数困惑度进⼀步降⾄ 0.68 ⾄ 0.70 ，模型可能展现出⽬前尚⽆法预⻅的新
能⼒。
不过，把困惑度作为共同标准，并不意味着问题已经解决。

### Page 3

帕乔茨基也承认，这⼀指标存在局限。
⾸先，各家公司的训练数据、数据配⽐和评估集不同，困惑度并不能直接⽤于横向⽐较。其
次，困惑度不能直接衡量模型在具体任务中的表现。⼀个模型的预测能⼒提⾼，不代表它在所
有数学、编程和安全测试中都会同步进步。此外，思维链推理等后训练技术也会改变模型能
⼒，却不⼀定直接反映在预训练困惑度上。
还有⼀个关键问题：困惑度存在理论下限。
随着模型越来越接近训练数据所包含的信息熵，损失下降可能逐渐放缓。但这不代表能⼒提升
也会停⽌。即使指标只发⽣很⼩的变化，也可能对应新的能⼒突破。
因此，帕乔茨基并不主张只⽤困惑度判断模型是否安全，⽽是希望把它与其他指标结合起来，
形成⼀套共同标准。为了推动这项⼯作， OpenAI 愿意公开更多预训练进展。
帕乔茨基认为，前沿模型公司应该共享必要的透明度信息，共同评估模型能⼒，并在红队测试
和安全标准⽅⾯开展合作。
03
OpenAI 与 Closed AI
帕乔茨基发表⽂章后，奥特曼很快公开表态⽀持。 “ 最近⼀段时间发布的最重要的事情。 ” 奥特曼

说。

### Page 4

奥特曼表⽰， OpenAI 正在提⾼预训练进展的透明度，希望能够与其他公司以合作伙伴的⾝份，
就共同限制达成⼀致。在他看来，这不仅有助于确保 AI 安全和可控，也能推动整个⾏业建⽴共
同规则。
对于帕乔茨基提出的困惑度限制，奥特曼也表⽰赞同。他希望各家实验室围绕困惑度及其他测
量指标建⽴共同约束，在研究⼈员真正具备理解和监控 AI ⾏为的能⼒之前，不跨越预先设定的能
⼒⻔槛。
他举出的例⼦，是开发下⼀代思维链监控器（ CoT Monitors ）。
在他看来，⾏业应该先明确第⼀道能⼒限制，再约定突破这道限制之前必须完成哪些安全⼯
作。
不过，奥特曼的⽀持，并没有消除外界的疑虑。⾸先受到质疑的是 OpenAI 推动⾏业共同设限的
动机。
⼀些⽹友认为，OpenAI 此时强调⾏业限制和安全标准，是否也有为⾃⾝模型竞争⼒争取时间的
考虑。有⼈认为，这些说法更像是模型发布不及竞争对⼿时使⽤的理由。
还有⼈讽刺奥特曼频繁使⽤ “ 透明度 ” ⼀词，称如果 OpenAI 真的希望提⾼透明度，就应该开源，

⽽不是继续忽视⽤⼾对模型连续性的要求。
批评还涉及 OpenAI 的公司定位。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Document 402: Q3 Wage Price Index_ award wage boost (2026-11-23)

### Page 1

#wages #inflation #labour
Q3 Wage Price Index: award wage boost
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
16:06 November 18 2026 Share
The minimum wage and award decision lifted wages growth, but unevenly – enterprise
arrangements responded more than individual arrangements, consistent with a softer
labour market. Q3 2026: 1.1%qtr, 3.5%yr.
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 1.1% in Q3 2026, slightly above Westpac’s forecast and the
market consensus for a 1.0% gain. That increased the annual growth rate to 3.5%yr – stronger than

### Page 2

Wages by Bargaining Stream
the past six months, but a similar pace to 2025.
Today’s data also showcased the impact of the 2026 Fair Work Commission’s Annual Wage Review,
which handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award wages. This lifted wages in
aggregate, but the gains were uneven.
A significant share of the increase was driven by health care and social assistance, reflecting pay
rises for state government workers covered by enterprise agreements.

In original terms, the WPI lifted 1.7% in Q3. Awards and enterprise arrangements accounted for 64%
of the lift, while individual arrangements contributed just 36%, the smallest share for Q3 since 2019.
Enterprise arrangements, which are often directly linked to award outcomes, were more responsive,
while individual arrangements were less so compared to previous September quarters. That is
consistent with a softer labour market and recent business survey evidence that suggests the rise in

private sector labour costs following the award decision quickly normalised.
Overall, we see the Q3 result as a step up in the level of wages from the award decision rather than
evidence of building underlying wage pressure but we will be watching whether the strength in
enterprise bargaining persists.

### Page 3

Westpac has long believed focusing on wage inflation by wage bargaining stream can help to
understand how wages respond to the economic cycle. Australia’s labour market is split into three
categories of pay setting methods:
Australian wage adjustments, including the Fair Work Commission’s (FWC) minimum wage and award
rate decisions, tend to occur at the start of the new financial year in July. The FWC handed down a
4.75% increase for 2026, larger than the decisions from 2025 (3.50%) and 2024 (3.75%), but lower

than in 2023 (5.75%).
In original terms, the WPI lifted 1.7% in Q3 2026. Of that increase, 0.50ppts were attributed to awards,
which was broadly in line with our expectations. However, both individual and enterprise arrangements
surprised us to the upside, contributing 0.64ppts and 0.65ppts to the quarterly increase in wages
respectively.
National minimum wage and award rates, regulated by the Fair Work Ombudsman,
Enterprise agreements, or
Individual arrangements.

### Page 4

Compared to the same quarter in previous years, each stream's contribution was the strongest in
absolute terms since 2023, largely because the overall lift was bigger than in 2024 and 2025. After
accounting for the size of the increase, enterprise arrangements were responsible for 64% of the lift,
whereas individual arrangements contributed just 36%, the smallest share since 2019.
While the larger award wage decision pushed up the overall increase, it did not flow through evenly.

Many enterprise agreements are directly linked to award outcomes, either through explicit reference to
award rates or because the award decision sets a benchmark in bargaining, which helps explain why
this stream was more responsive. By contrast, wages set under individual arrangements can be more
discretionary and depend more on employers' circumstances, so they were less responsive than in
previous years. That may reflect a softer labour market, where weaker demand for workers reduces the

pressure on employers to lift pay.
Wages by Sector
By sector, private wages (excl. bonuses) rose 1.0% in Q3, pulling up the annual pace from 3.1%yr to
3.4%yr. This marks the strongest pace of wages growth in the private sector since 2023, at the time of
the Fair Work Commission’s decision to raise award wages by a much larger 5.75%.
Private sector pay is set through a mix of arrangements, including a significant share of individual
arrangements (as discussed above). Today's result suggests this year's award wage decision may

have had some flow-on effect to the broader private sector, though, as noted, the pass-through was
uneven and appears to have been stronger for enterprise arrangements than for individual
arrangements.
There was also an element of both breadth and scale when it comes to private sector wage gains. Half
of all private sector jobs recorded a wage increase in Q3, the largest share since this series began in
2016; and of those jobs that recorded an increase, average hourly wages rose 4.5%, the strongest

since Q3 2023 (5.8%).
Public wages continue to outpace the private sector, this time with a 1.3% lift in Q3, bringing the annual
growth pace to 3.9%yr, the strongest since Q4 2025. As expected, the bulk of the gain was driven by
pay rises under enterprise agreements at the state government level, particularly for front-line workers.
Additionally, given scheduled rises under existing APS-wide enterprise agreements for Commonwealth
employees had already been delivered in previous quarters, the Commonwealth level contribution to

public sector wages growth during Q3 was modest.
Relative to the private sector, the driver of public sector wage gains looks to be more about scale than
breadth – across the 38% of public sector jobs recording a wage increase during the quarter the
average hourly wage change was 4.0%, slightly under the record high of a 4.3% recorded back in Q4
2023.

### Page 5

Summary
The Q3 result shows that the larger minimum wage and award rate decision boosted wages but the
detail suggests the lift was driven more by workers with stronger bargaining power. Awards and
enterprise arrangements, where pay outcomes are more directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s
decision or to collective bargaining, whereas individual arrangements, which are more exposed to
labour market conditions, contributed the smallest share since 2019.

That is consistent with a softer labour market – the unemployment rate has risen ½ppt since the start of
the year, and we are expecting it to peak at 5.2% mid next year. The NAB business survey points the
same way, with the increase in labour costs reported by private sector firms following the award
decision quickly normalising. This suggests the Q3 result is better read as a temporary step up in the
level of wages from the award wage decision than as evidence of building underlying wage pressure,

though we will be watching whether the strength in enterprise bargaining persists.

Source: US Energy Information Administration, Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *EIA projections

Editorial 
Staying diversified, adding US mega caps 
Strategy summary. AI-related concerns continue to weigh on 
equities, especially with new US restrictions on third-country 
access to advanced American semiconductor chips. Renewed 
volatility across the AI ecosystem drove the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) back above 25. However, two major event risks this 
week turned out to be less concerning - Nvidia’s Q3 revenue 
and earnings exceeded expectations, while Anthropic’s record 
initial public offering was priced above its indicative range.  
Our quant models are not signalling strong buy signal yet. 
However, with VIX spiking above 25 and investor positioning 
and sentiment indicators suggesting limited downside, we see 
value emerging in the US mega caps segment following its 
recent underperformance. We add an opportunistic idea on the 
segment, which we see benefitting from scale, financial 
strength and potential industry consolidation.  
Opportunistically adding US megacaps. Asian equities led 
declines this week, led by Taiwan, China and Korea, after new 
US export controls targeted access to advanced AI computing 
through overseas data centres. Licensing requirements focus 
on capacity supplied to restricted customers, with additional 
controls affecting regional hubs. These measures increase 
compliance burdens and financing uncertainty, potentially 
weighing on AI capital expenditure. We expect the market’s AI 
capex estimates for 2027 to be cut towards our own more-
conservative projections. Established cloud operators 
(hyperscalers) may be better equipped to manage customer 
checks than smaller providers. We initiate an opportunistic idea 
on US mega caps as we see a catch-up opportunity, given their 
established franchises, strong cash generation and relatively 
resilient earnings prospects (see page 7 for details). 
Diversifying beyond the AI trade. Although positive cross 
asset and sector correlations returned this week, sector 
rotations earlier this month reinforces the importance of 
maintaining exposure to different drivers of equity returns. Our 
preferred US sectors remain technology, healthcare, financials 
and materials, combining technology participation with 
opportunities beyond AI. Beyond the US, we like Euro area and 
Japanese banks, which are benefitting from elevated interest 
rates, actively managed global dividend strategies for income 
opportunities, and Indian equities due to their low correlation 
with the AI trade. 
Allure of high dividend equities. While global dividend yields 
remain historically low, attractive pockets persist. The Swiss 
equity market yields almost 4%, versus 1.6% for global equities. 
Euro area (3.6%), Australia (3.5%) and Singapore (3.4%) are 
other leading dividend paying markets. Taiwan offers almost a 
2% yield, combining income with structural growth 
opportunities. We favour active management over simply 
chasing yield, avoiding value traps. Dividend equities can 
complement bonds within a diversified portfolio, balancing 
income opportunities with broader exposure across markets. 
Increasing diversification offered by bonds. The one-month 
correlation between global stocks and bonds has turned 
negative after more than a year. This means bonds are offering 
greater diversification benefits amid the equity drawdown. 
Minutes from the Fed’s October meeting suggest policymakers 
are likely to hike rates further. However, we remain dovish vs. 
markets on the Fed’s 12-month rates outlook (we see Fed 
target rate at 4.25% by end-2027 vs. market pricing above 
4.5%) as lower oil prices and fading tariff effects lead to 
noticeable disinflation by H2 2027.  Thus, we see particular 
value in the 3-7-year maturity bucket, with a preference for BB-
to-BBB segment corporate bonds, which have resilient balance 
sheets and offer attractive yield pick-up. We see the US 10-year 
government bond offering value if the yield rises above 5.25%.  
Dollar appears overbought; potential for gold upside: The 
USD index (DXY) has jumped over 2% in the past two weeks 
after stronger-than-expected US jobs and inflation reports for 
October revived Fed rate hike bets. Nevertheless, crude oil 
prices have slumped almost 20% this month as the Republican 
party’s heavy losses in the US mid-term elections raised the 
prospects of de-escalation in the Middle East conflict. Forward 
markets are pricing US crude oil below USD 75/bbl by Q3 next 
year. Lower oil prices are likely to support disinflation next year. 
With DXY technically overbought and Fed policy expectations 
excessively hawkish, we expect a pullback in USD. Conversely, 
gold looks oversold. We see an upside for XAU/USD, despite 
revising lower our 3- and 12-month targets (see page 7).   
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 20 November 2026

Top client questions 
  How will the new US semiconductor restrictions impact 
the technology sector?  
Our view: While compute is likely to remain a bottleneck for AI, 
greater US regulatory restrictions could weigh on AI capex.   
Rationale: The US has announced broader restrictions on overseas 
computing capacity used by Chinese and other restricted-country 
companies, extending controls beyond physical chip shipments to 
remote access through data centres. This could delay data centre 
deployments and raise financing costs, as affected operators may 
defer expansion plans until licences or replacement customers are 
secured. Lower spending could weigh on chip and hardware orders, 
bringing consensus capex estimates closer to our forecasts. While 
we expect AI capex to rise 50% to USD 1.2trn in 2027, supported 
by projects already under construction, the compound annual growth 
rate (CAGR) between 2027 and 2030 is likely to moderate to c.12%.  
We therefore favour broadening technology exposure beyond 
semiconductors through our US Information Technology (IT) 
opportunistic idea, which also includes hardware and software 
industries. Investors should also consider diversifying into our other 
preferred sectors, such as US healthcare and financials.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  How were Chinese technology giants' Q3 2026 earnings? 
Our view: Chinese tech giants’ Q3 earnings highlight growing 
demand for AI, but heavy spending is pressuring margins and 
cash flow, underscoring the need for stronger AI monetisation. 
Rationale: Heavy AI capex continues to weigh on profitability, 
compressing near-term margins and deepening negative free cash 
flows, with share dilution a risk as tech giants may have to tap into 
equity markets for the AI buildout. Cloud growth points to genuine AI 
demand, but the AI-related revenue contribution remains small 
relative to the cost base. AI monetisation remains challenging, with 
competition set to intensify. Weak consumer spending was a 
common thread contributing to revenue misses. Major players 
reiterated the need for more tangible investment returns amid 
aggressive AI investments. 
In the near term, investor sentiment remains weighed down by 
tightening US restrictions targeting China’s access to advanced 
chips, introducing downside risks to 2027 AI cloud revenue. While 
this could hamper the development of next-generation AI models, 
accelerating domestic AI chip adoption should benefit local 
semiconductor companies as China pushes for technological self-
sufficiency. We expect rising AI adoption to benefit China's IT sector 
over time, though we prefer diversifying our China equity exposure 
across preferred sectors, such as financials and healthcare. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

AI capital expenditure (capex) should continue to 
rise, but growth is expected to moderate from 2028

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 20 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the implications of the October Fed meeting 
minutes for the rate path forward and short-dated bonds? Do 
US 10-year government bonds now offer a good re-entry point? 
Our view: October’s Fed meeting minutes maintained a 
hawkish-hold narrative. We continue to expect the Fed to raise 
rates by 25bps at its 8-9 December meeting. However, with 
short-dated US government bonds having largely priced in 
three hikes by June 2027, we see any spike above 5.25% in the 
US 10-year bond yield as an opportunistic bullish entry point.  
Rationale: The hawkish-hold tone of the Fed’s October meeting 
minutes suggests the December Fed meeting is ‘live’. The market is 
currently pricing in three hikes by year-end 2027 (vs. our 
expectation of two hikes by H1 2027 followed by a potential cut 
in H2 2027). Short-dated government bond yields – using the US 2-
year yield as a proxy – have largely priced in three hikes, and we do 
not expect the hawkish minutes to have a material impact on them.   
Should the US 10-year government bond yield spike above 5.25%, 
the near-term risk-reward would become favourable, in our opinion. 
As we expect inflation to moderate in H2 2027, we see scope for 
the Fed to pivot towards easing, and we would turn more 
constructive on short-dated government bonds. We expect the US 
2-year government bond yield to decline to 4.00-4.25% in 12 
months, contributing to yield curve steepening. 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Is it time to add exposure to the EUR after its recent slide 
against the USD? 
Our view: Yes, we are bullish on EUR/USD and favour gradually 
adding EUR exposure in the coming weeks. Markets are pricing 
in more Fed tightening than we expect. Lower oil prices support 
the Euro area and are likely to ease the Fed’s inflation concerns 
on a forward-looking basis. 
Rationale: Lower oil prices are a double positive for EUR/USD, 
as they ease costs for Euro area households and businesses and 
temper US inflation, reducing the need for the Fed to tighten as 
much as markets expect.  
Markets are pricing in a terminal Fed funds rate close to 4.63%, down 
from 4.72% a week ago, with the yield curve now also pricing a rate-
cutting cycle after the September 2027 peak. We expect the bullish 
near-term USD momentum to linger until markets’ peak rate 
expectations converge towards our view. EUR/USD’s relative 
strength index (RSI) is below 30, and consolidation above 1.0950 
points to oversold conditions. We expect a rebound towards 1.11 
to confirm our recovery view in the next three weeks. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist
